# BACKLOG.md — Sublore build plan

Ordered milestones for v1.0 (scope: CONTRIBUTING.md §1). Rules: work top to bottom; a milestone's tasks are detailed when the milestone starts, using M0 as the template; every task gets acceptance criteria BEFORE implementation; the owner's checklist at the end of each milestone is the definition of done.

Status legend: `[ ]` open · `[~]` in progress · `[x]` verified-by-tests · `[✓]` owner-passed · `[!]` blocked

Behavioural verdicts in this file are **verified on Linux** unless they say otherwise. Windows compiles in CI; it is not verified until MW closes (CONTRIBUTING.md platform policy, 2026-08-29).

The planning documents this file cites — `decisions.md`, `shell-layout.md`, `shell-mockup.html`, `m2-0-tasks.md`, `post-v1-plan.md`, `x11-vs-render-api.md` and the ASR research sweeps — left this repository on 2026-09-01 for the owner's private planning archive. They are named here, never given a path: `docs/design/` no longer exists.

**Decisions in force** (owner ruling 2026-08-29, full text in `decisions.md`):

| # | Decision | Lands in |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Video occlusion: native surface hides for HTML layers | M2.0 |
| 2 | Show and re-show with a loaded video, built now | N2 |
| 3 | Windows E2E backend | MW.1a (done), MW.1b, MW.1c |
| 4 | Composite history entry, one undo per operation | M2.5 (shape), M7 (use) |
| 5 | Active line and selection separated | M2.0 |
| 6 | Text matcher in the open core | M5 |
| 7 | Video shows the translation, source on toggle, shadow copy | M2.6 |
| 8 | Autosave in its own store, backups untouchable by timers | M11 (post-v1) |
| 9 | Close gate — active defect | N1 |
| 10 | Non-cue segments: written analysis now | N3 |
| 11 | Milliseconds, final. No frame vocabulary | M2.5 note, plan-wide |
| 12 | M2.4 reuses the ASR pattern, not its code | M2.4 |
| 13 | New translation from source | M2.6 |

---

## M0 — Skeleton that proves the stack (fully specified, start here)

Goal: a running Tauri 2 app on Windows and Linux that plays a video with libmpv and has green CI. This milestone exists to kill stack risk first: if libmpv embedding fails, we learn it in week one, not month three.

- [~] **M0.1 Repo bootstrap.** Tauri 2 + Rust core + TS frontend, workspace layout, lint/format hooks, README stub.
  - AC: `npm run tauri dev` opens a window titled Sublore on Windows and Linux; CI runs lint + build on both and is green.
  - Status 2026-08-22: implementation merged to `main` locally; full check battery (install, format, lint, build, fmt, clippy, cargo build) plus live app launch with window title verified on Linux. Remote publication deferred by owner, so the CI-green and Windows halves of the AC are unverified.
- [~] **M0.2 libmpv embedding.** Video renders inside the app window via libmpv, with play/pause and seek.
  - AC: open fixture `fixtures/video/sample.mkv`; video plays with audio; pause, seek to 0:30, frame changes accordingly; app closes cleanly with no orphan process.
  - Status 2026-08-22: implemented (libmpv2 crate, X11 wid embedding via native child window, XWayland on Wayland); 14 behavioral tests green against real libmpv; independent live verification under Xvfb passed every AC (video advances, pause freezes byte-identical, seek lands at 30.0, WM_DELETE_WINDOW close exits 0 with no orphans). Audio verified to decode (AAC track selected); audibility needs the owner's speakers. Windows and CI runs still unverified (no remote, no Windows machine).
- [~] **M0.3 Packaging smoke.** CI produces installable artifacts (msi/exe, deb + AppImage).
  - AC: artifact from CI installs and runs M0.2's behavior on a clean VM of each OS.
  - **The AC splits by platform and only the Linux half can be answered today.** A `package-linux` job builds one bundle per runner and uploads one artifact each, deb, rpm and AppImage; a `package-windows` job builds the msi and the exe. A `package-smoke` job installs the deb in a clean `ubuntu:24.04` container, starts what it installed and then the AppImage beside it under Xvfb, and a `package-smoke-rpm` job does the same for the rpm in a clean `fedora:latest`. Both are red unless the 1024x700 "Sublore" toplevel comes up mapped. It is `.github/scripts/package-smoke.sh` and a Node check that reads the window through `e2e/lib/x11.js`, the same seam MW.1b replaces. Nothing installs or starts the Windows artifacts: no Windows behaviour has ever been run here, so the msi and the exe are files this workflow produces and nothing more. **Installing and running them is MW.3's**, by hand, from the installed build. One thing to expect there, and it is no longer read off the config: it was run. On 2026-09-05 the Windows exe was built for the gnu target on the owner's Linux machine and started under wine 11.0 in a folder holding nothing but itself, which is what an installer that ships no resources produces. It does not start, and it names `WebView2Loader.dll` and `libmpv-2.dll`, `status c0000135`. **Half of that is a property of the gnu build and not of what ships.** The runner's own MSVC `sublore.exe` imports 27 DLLs and `libmpv-2.dll` is among them while `WebView2Loader.dll` is not: the webview loader is reached at run time there, not through the import table. So the prediction this line made was right, one library and not two, and the second name is the mingw linker's and has to be read as such. `tauri.conf.json` declares no `resources`, no `externalBin` and no `bundle.windows` key at all, so neither is shipped. Everything else the exe imports is a system library. This is a wine trace of a mingw build and not a Windows run, so it names what MW.3 will hit and is not a Windows verdict; what it saves is the owner finding it by hand.
  - Status 2026-09-02, verified on Linux, locally and not in CI: the path was replicated in containers rather than trusted. An `ubuntu:24.04` container with the workflow's apt list built both bundles, and a second, clean `ubuntu:24.04` installed the deb and started both artifacts, 3 checks each. The smoke was proven to bite: a deb built without the libmpv dependency installs and then dies with `libmpv.so.2: cannot open shared object file` (exit 127), and a missing binary and a window title the app does not have both fail too. **CI has never run any of this** — nothing has been pushed — so the artifact names, the `container:` job and the upload/download pair are unverified.
  - Status 2026-09-03, unverified: the three Linux bundles were split onto a runner each and the rpm was added (owner ruling). The rpm bundler is `rpm-rs` inside the Tauri CLI, so no `rpmbuild` is needed, and it writes `libwebkit2gtk-4.1.so.0()(64bit)` and `libgtk-3.so.0()(64bit)` into Requires by itself but nothing for libmpv; `tauri.conf.json` declares that one by soname, which is what both Fedora's `mpv-libs` and openSUSE's `libmpv2` provide. Built and its Requires read on Fedora 44 locally. **Nothing has installed or started an rpm anywhere**, so `package-smoke-rpm` is a check nobody has seen pass.
  - Found on the way: the deb declared no libmpv dependency at all, so it installed anywhere and then failed to start; `tauri.conf.json` now declares `libmpv2`. The AppImage needs `librsvg2-dev` at build time for its gtk plugin, and leaves `libOpenGL.so.0` to the host, which a bare image has to install. Building the AppImage on Fedora also needs `NO_STRIP=true`, because linuxdeploy's bundled strip is older than `.relr.dyn`; the runner's Ubuntu does not, so the workflow does not set it.
- [~] **M0.4 Crash safety baseline.** Panic/error handler; log file in app data dir; no console spam in release.
  - AC: forced error path shows an actionable dialog and writes the log; app restarts normally after.
  - Input from M0.2 (2026-08-22): an external client hard-destroying the toplevel via XDestroyWindow segfaults the app in libgobject (normal WM_DELETE_WINDOW close is clean, exit 0). Not a user-reachable path through normal close; assess and handle or explicitly accept during this task.
  - Status 2026-08-23: implemented and verified live on Linux. Forced panic writes a crash report, shows a native dialog, exits 101; normal close exits 0 with no report; the app relaunches cleanly afterwards; release build emits nothing on stdout. XDestroyWindow segfault explicitly accepted and recorded in README (a panic hook cannot see SIGSEGV, and at M0 the app writes no user data). Windows paths and CI unverified: no remote, no Windows machine.
- [~] **M0.5 E2E smoke harness.** tauri-driver + WebDriver behavioral test asserting the app launches with window title Sublore, wired into CI. Filed from M0.1: title verification there was manual (Xvfb + xdotool), CONTRIBUTING.md §5.2 wants it automated.
  - AC: a CI job on Linux runs the E2E smoke; the test fails if the window title is not "Sublore"; removing the harness or skipping the test turns CI red.
  - AC (filed from M0.2): the same job opens `fixtures/video/sample.mkv` and fails if the native video surface is not sized over the stage element, and fails if closing the window leaves a non-zero exit status or a surviving process. M0.2's suite drives the player headless only, so `video::setup` and `VideoSurface::create` have no automated coverage today.
  - Status 2026-08-23: harness implemented in `e2e/` (tauri-driver + WebdriverIO + Mocha) with four checks: window title, fixture opened, native video surface sized over the stage, close with exit 0 and no survivors. Suite verified green locally and proven to fail when each assertion is broken. A screen smaller than the 1024x700 window makes the video checks fail, so the Xvfb size is pinned in CI. CI job never executed: no remote.
  - Harness note from M0.2: `xdotool search --name Sublore` also matches GTK's 10x10 group-leader window and lists it first, so select the toplevel by its 1024x700 geometry. Close it with an ICCCM-conformant WM_DELETE_WINDOW ClientMessage (python-xlib helper): `xdotool windowquit` proved a no-op in this WM-less Xvfb environment, and `xdotool windowclose` is XDestroyWindow, which bypasses the app's close path entirely (and currently segfaults it, see M0.4).

**Owner checklist M0:** install from CI artifact on your machine → open a video → play, pause, seek → close → reopen. Everything behaves; nothing left running in task manager.

## M1 — Subtitle formats, lossless (tasks detailed 2026-08-23; implemented and verified-by-tests 2026-08-28)

Goal: open and save SRT, ASS, VTT without destroying anything, including files from the wild.

- [x] **M1.1 Subtitle core model + SRT.** Internal document model that preserves what it does not understand (raw payloads, ordering, quirks); SRT parser + serializer; the subtitle fixture tree is born here (`fixtures/subtitles/`, committed, with a `.gitattributes` rule keeping it byte-exact).
  - AC: every clean SRT fixture round-trips parse → serialize byte-for-byte (CRLF/LF mixes, BOM, blank-line quirks, numbering gaps, overlapping cues, non-Latin scripts); every malformed fixture yields a structured error with line number and reason, never a panic, never a silent fix.
- [x] **M1.2 VTT lossless.** WEBVTT parser + serializer: header, NOTE/STYLE/REGION blocks, cue settings, voice/inline tags preserved raw.
  - AC: same byte-stable round-trip guarantee over `fixtures/subtitles/vtt/`; malformed → structured error, never a panic.
- [x] **M1.3 ASS lossless.** .ass/.ssa parser + serializer preserving section order, Format lines, styles, and override tags as raw text.
  - AC: same byte-stable round-trip guarantee over `fixtures/subtitles/ass/`; malformed → structured error, never a panic.
- [x] **M1.4 Atomic save + rolling backups.** Temp file + fsync + rename per CONTRIBUTING.md §3; before overwriting an existing subtitle file, a timestamped backup is kept (rolling cap as designed); no other code path deletes backups.
  - AC: a crash-injection behavioral test interrupts the save repeatedly and the destination is always either the old or the new content in full, never truncated or mixed; overwriting an existing file always leaves a timestamped backup; the rolling cap is enforced and tested.
- [x] **M1.5 Open/save wired into the app.** Minimal UI: open a subtitle file, show format + cue count, save-as; parse errors surface as readable UI messages.
  - AC: E2E: open an SRT fixture → format and cue count visible; save-as of a clean fixture produces a byte-identical file; open a malformed fixture → readable error message, app still alive and usable.

**Status 2026-08-28:** all five tasks implemented on `main`. Two new workspace crates: `sublore-formats` (lossless model + SRT/VTT/ASS) and `sublore-io` (atomic save, rolling backups, crash injection). 43 clean fixtures round-trip byte-for-byte and 23 malformed fixtures produce structured errors, verified independently in release profile as well as by the suites; 182 Rust tests and 7 E2E checks green. A release-only silent-corruption path found in review (the tiling guard was behind `debug_assert!`, plus a multi-byte character split that emptied the file) is fixed and covered by tests. Windows and CI unverified: no remote, no Windows machine.

**Owner checklist M1:** open a real .srt from your disk → cue count appears; save-as → the copy is byte-identical (fc/cmp); open a deliberately broken file → clear error, app keeps working.

## NOW — ahead of every functional milestone (owner ruling 2026-08-29)

Order updated 2026-08-30 by owner ruling: N2c closes the NOW block, then gate 2, then M2.0 from its first task. Decision 1 is no longer ahead of the gate — it sits inside M2.0, after the shell work it depends on, as an acceptance criterion there.

Two items jump the queue by owner decision. Full reasoning in `decisions.md`; the ruling settles all thirteen questions raised in `post-v1-plan.md`. Order is fixed: N1, then N2, then M2.0.

- [x] **N1 Close gate (decision 9).** Intercept `CloseRequested`; if anything is unsaved, ask save / discard / cancel per dirty document and honour the answer.
  - Status 2026-08-29, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: a native three-button dialog. Native rather than HTML because the video surface raises above the webview on every region update, so an HTML dialog would sit behind the video until decision 1 lands, and N1 has to stand on its own. `e2e/scripts/close-gate-check.js` drives all three answers end to end, 12 checks, and runs in CI beside the shutdown check; `shutdown-check.js` gained a fifth check proving no gate is raised over a document nobody edited. Save and discard resolve the dirty state before closing, so the close that follows finds nothing to ask and cannot loop; a save that fails closes nothing and says why. Two review passes: the first found 3 blockers, 8 serious, 9 minor; the second found 1 blocker, 5 serious, 10 minor. Owner checklist still to run.
  - Small debt, filed 2026-08-29: `close-gate-check.js` still uses fixed waits between opening the file and double-clicking a row, because without a DOM there is nothing observable there to wait on. When M2.0 gives those points something observable, replace them with `waitFor`.
  - Known gap, filed not fixed: an inline cue editor holding uncommitted text leaves the backend session clean, so the window closes without asking and that text is lost. Covering it needs the gate to consult the frontend, which is the HTML-dialog shape decision 1 will settle. Out of scope here.
  - AC: open a subtitle fixture, edit a cue, close the window: a dialog appears offering save, discard and cancel. Cancel leaves the app open with the edit still there and the file on disk untouched. Discard closes and leaves the file untouched. Save writes the edit and then closes.
  - AC: with nothing modified, closing the window exits straight away with status 0 and no dialog, and the existing shutdown checks still pass unchanged.
- [x] **N2 Video surface show and re-show (decision 2).** Build the re-show path for the native surface with a video already loaded. Prerequisite of M2.0's occlusion handling (decision 1).
  - AC: open a video, hide the surface, show it again: the frame is visible and playback continues. The assertion is on the visible frame, not on an internal flag, because mpv creates its own window inside ours and leaves it unmapped if ours is (`video/surface/mod.rs:98-99`) — a state-only assertion would pass while the user sees black.
  - **Correction, gate 2 (2026-08-30):** the automated suite does not assert the visible frame. `062f201` rewrote `video-surface.spec.js` and replaced the pixel/saturation check this AC describes with map-state and `childWindows().length > 0` checks; the file's own header says so ("What is asserted here is the surface coming back mapped with mpv still attached to it — not the pixels"). That change was never named in `062f201`'s delivery description, and CONTRIBUTING.md §5.4 requires a weakened assertion to be said out loud. The reasoning in this AC still holds — it is why the suite checks mpv's mapped child window instead of an internal flag — but the pixel half of "the frame is visible" is verified by hand only, three launches on the owner's own Wayland session showing the picture at saturation 5.86 against 2.1 for the empty shell, not by anything CI runs.
  - AC: hide and re-show ten times in a row leaves no orphan process and no leaked surface.
  - Status 2026-08-30, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: visibility is now derived, never set. One `SurfaceState` in `video/mod.rs` holds whether a video is open and whether the last reported rectangle has area, and a single `settle` turns that into show or hide; nothing else in the module touches the window. Geometry moves the surface and never decides its visibility, which is what keeps the empty stage uncovered and lets a rectangle that went empty come back. Each open carries a generation, so an older open's failure cannot clear a newer one. Two specs: `video-surface.spec.js` (playing, paused with no nudge, ten cycles) and `video-empty.spec.js` (startup, layout change, failed open), 33 E2E checks in total from 27. Pixels are measured with ffmpeg rather than ImageMagick, whose `magick` binary does not exist on the CI runner.
  - Gate 1 (2026-08-30): three review passes found 7 blockers, 15 serious, 25 minor; all fixed. The heaviest: the fix for the first pass had made the surface visible at startup with no video, and the "clock is frozen" assertion could not fail because `waitFor` returns on its first evaluation.
- [x] **N2b libmpv attaches inside a Wayland session (product defect, filed 2026-08-29).** In a Wayland session libmpv does not attach to the X11 surface it is handed: the surface reports `IsViewable` with zero children, the stage keeps its placeholder, and the transport happily reports playback over a picture that is not there. `main.rs:9` already forces `GDK_BACKEND=x11` before `gtk_init`, so GTK is not the culprit — the N2 probe's first diagnosis blamed it and was wrong. The component ignoring the `wid` is libmpv, which `player.rs:186-187` never pins to an X11 output. **This is a foundation defect on the declared primary platform:** CONTRIBUTING.md names Linux primary, and the owner's machine is Fedora in a Wayland session, so today the app only works there by accident of the environment. Runs after N2 merges and before decision 1.
  - AC: with `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` set — the owner's real session, not a cleaned one — open `fixtures/video/sample.mkv`: mpv's own window exists inside the native surface, and the surface shows a picture. Both are asserted, because the surface reports `IsViewable` either way.
  - AC: the same run leaves no orphan process and closes with status 0.
  - **CONTRIBUTING.md section 7, measured 2026-08-31 on the owner's machine** (owner ruling 4), launch and capture only, no synthetic input. Cold start to a painted window: 1095, 1076 and 460 ms armed; 1112 and 988 ms disarmed. Idle PSS sixty seconds after opening the fixture with the video paused: 142 MB armed, 148 and 221 MB disarmed. Against budgets of 2 s and 400 MB, **both are met in both configurations**, so the mitigation is accepted on cost as well as on effect.
  - Caveat on that measurement, recorded rather than smoothed: the disarmed runs are reported here as painting, while `webview-paint-check.js` measures a disarmed window as flat with a luma range of 0. The budget script detects paint with ImageMagick min/max over the captured window, which on a live compositor also catches the frame; the dedicated check uses a luma range against a measured threshold. The paint verdict of the dedicated check is the one to trust. The memory figures do not depend on the difference.
  - Note: the harness keeps clearing `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` (`e2e/lib/env.js`) for determinism, but that is hygiene, not the fix, and the comment there says so. This task's test must not use it.
  - Status 2026-08-30, verified-by-tests **on Linux**, and separately verified by running the app **on the owner's own Wayland session**: libmpv now pins `gpu-context=x11egl` whenever a `wid` is handed to it. `wayland-attach-check.js` runs with `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` set and passes the fixture as a command-line argument rather than typing it, so the input race below cannot reach it; the check discriminates, measured by deleting the option and rebuilding, which leaves the surface childless. On the real session three launches out of three showed the picture, saturation 5.86 against 2.1 for the empty shell.
  - Two defects were found on the way and both are fixed here, because the app was unusable on the primary platform without them. WebKitGTK could not allocate through DMABUF on this driver and the window opened entirely blank; `main.rs` now applies the documented escalation before the webview exists, with `SUBLORE_WEBKIT_WORKAROUNDS=0` as an escape hatch. And the workarounds cost input latency — 373 ms to reach React state against 186 ms without — which is what made `asr.spec.js` fail under Xvfb; the harness sets the escape hatch and the app keeps no knowledge of its test environment.
  - Two more were found and filed rather than fixed here: N2c, the surface misplaced under fractional scaling, and N1b, an intermittent SIGSEGV on exit after the close gate saves. Neither is caused by this change.
  - `startup_files` in `lib.rs` opens whatever is named on the command line. It exists because synthetic keystrokes go to whichever window holds the X focus, and during the N2b check on the owner's live session they landed in his own window and typed a fixture path into it.

- [x] **N2c Video surface is misplaced under fractional scaling (fixed 2026-08-30).** On the owner's display — 3840x2160 at scale 1.5 — the video plays but lands at a fraction of its rectangle, over the transcription bar instead of the stage.
  - **The mechanism, measured 2026-08-30 on that display:** the page reports `getBoundingClientRect()` in CSS pixels (`VideoStage.tsx:26-33`) and one CSS pixel here is 1.5 X pixels — `devicePixelRatio` is 1.5, measured on that display. The Linux path at the time passed those numbers through untouched (`surface/linux.rs:63-64` called `logical()`, since renamed away — see below), so the surface landed at 1/1.5 of its position and size. The first explanation, that `apply_region` mishandled a fractional `scale_factor()`, was wrong and is withdrawn.
  - **The multiplier could not come from the backend.** `window.scale_factor()` is an `AtomicI32` in `tao` and reports 1.0 on this display, so the old `physical()` would have multiplied by 1 and `logical()` by nothing. Only the page knows the ratio. That made this a change to what crosses the IPC boundary, and CONTRIBUTING.md section 6 applies: the region contract is a public interface and the Windows path moved in the same change or it would double-scale, since `scale_factor()` there does carry the ratio and the old `physical()` already multiplied by it. **Correction, gate 2:** the fix (`c7261a5`) deleted `logical()`, `physical()` and their shared `scaled()` helper from `surface/mod.rs`, replacing them with `pixels()` and `pixels_over(divisor)`; the two paragraphs above describe the pre-fix code, kept here as the diagnosis history, not the current API.
  - AC: on the owner's display at scale 1.5, the surface covers the stage. Verified by a screenshot of the app's own window, and measured: the surface's X geometry equals the stage rectangle times `devicePixelRatio`, within a pixel.
  - AC: a unit test pins the conversion, covering a fractional ratio and the 16-bit X11 clamp, and runs in CI where no fractional display exists.
  - AC: the E2E suite stays green under Xvfb, where `devicePixelRatio` is 1 and the change must be a no-op. A green suite there is necessary and not sufficient, and the delivery says so.
  - Status 2026-08-30, verified **on the owner's 1.5 display** and by tests on Linux: the page resolves the rectangle to native pixels before it crosses the IPC boundary, and each platform undoes only what it is about to redo — Linux divides by the GDK window's own scale factor because GDK re-applies it, Windows takes the numbers as they are. The factor never travels on the wire. Measured on the real display: surface 592x180 at 432,500, which is the stage's CSS rectangle times 1.5, against 395x120 before. Unit tests cover 1, 1.5 and 2 on both paths plus the 16-bit clamp; `e2e/scripts/scaled-surface-check.js` guards the 4x regression the first attempt nearly shipped and, as its own header says, does **not** prove N2c — a fractional ratio cannot be produced under Xvfb. Windows compiles in CI; the Windows path above has never been run.
  - **Correction, gate 2 (2026-08-30):** the first AC asks for a screenshot, and all the screenshot evidence ever amounted to was one sentence saying a capture of the window showed the picture inside the stage — no capture command named, no image committed to the repo, so it cannot be re-run by anyone. The measured half of that AC — the surface geometry, 592x180 at 432,500 — is independently re-checkable and was re-derived from the page's own formula during gate 2; the picture half was seen once and is not re-checkable.
  - Open suspect, measured once while diagnosing N2c and never explained: twelve seconds after launch the toplevel measured 800x600 while the startup log recorded an inner size of 1024x700. Something resized the window and nothing measured what. If the geometry work turns the cause up, it gets written down; if it does not, it stays on the record as open rather than being quietly dropped.
- [x] **N1b The window segfaults on exit after the close gate saves, under load (fixed 2026-08-30).** The main thread dies inside `_gdk_x11_display_queue_events` one loop iteration after `close_window` asked for `window.destroy()`. Two hypotheses have been measured and killed: rfd's second GTK thread (the crash survives its removal, with a core showing one GTK thread and no rfd frame) and "the sequential rate is high" (sixty sequential runs, thirty per branch, produced nothing at all).
  - **The conditions are now measured.** It does not reproduce sequentially. Under six concurrent streams it reproduces at 2 in 30 on the **save** branch and 0 in 30 on discard, same binary, same probe, load the only variable. Save and discard leave by the identical path and differ in one thing: save writes the file and its backup on the worker thread first, which delays the destroy, and load lengthens that delay. The next attempt aims there, not at "save is special".
  - No data was at risk in anything observed: the file is written and the backup kept before the crash, which lands on the way out.
  - AC: **sixty save-branch runs of `e2e/scripts/n1b-load-probe.js` in six concurrent streams, zero SIGSEGV and zero core dumps.** At the measured rate an unfixed defect survives that battery about one time in sixty. The old criterion, thirty sequential clean runs, is retired: it is now known to be unable to fail.
  - AC: thirty sequential runs of `pnpm e2e:close-gate` stay clean, and no assertion in it is weakened. It guards the behaviour even though it cannot prove the fix.
  - **Correction, gate 2 (2026-08-30):** this AC was not met by the evidence the checkbox below certifies. Sequential `pnpm e2e:close-gate` was run 30 green, 0 red on the pre-fix binary and 3 green, 0 red on the binary that shipped, so the delivered binary was run three times sequentially, not thirty, and the gap was never closed by running the remaining twenty-seven or by lowering this line to match. The number to trust for the delivered binary is three, not thirty.
  - CI: the runner is small and busy, which is the condition under which this reproduces, so the close gate check may go red there. It stays armed: it is catching a real crash.
  - Status 2026-08-30, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: `close_window` asks for the close instead of destroying the GTK window behind tao's back, and the gate's answer travels in a single-use `CLOSING` flag so the resulting close request passes without asking again. `asr` and the video surface shut down in `CloseRequested`, where every other close already does it. Judged on the delivered binary: 60 save-branch runs in six concurrent streams with 0 SIGSEGV and 0 cores, against 2 in 30 before; close gate 12/12 three times; shutdown 5/5. At the previous rate an unfixed defect survives that battery about one time in sixty, which is why this is written as "the crash did not occur in the battery built to make it occur" and not as a death certificate.
  - Two hypotheses were measured and killed on the way, and both stay on the record rather than being quietly dropped: rfd's second GTK thread, killed by a crash that survived its removal with a core showing one GTK thread and no rfd frame, and a high sequential rate, killed by sixty sequential runs that produced nothing at all.
- [x] **N1c The file picker still starts rfd's second GTK thread (latent defect, filed 2026-08-30).** Not the cause of N1b — that hypothesis is refuted — but unsound on its own terms: rfd spawns a permanent thread that iterates GTK for the rest of the process's life, and GTK3 is not built to be driven from two threads. N1b removed the close gate's three message dialogs from that path; `project::choose_path` (`project/mod.rs:245-257`) still calls `blocking_pick_folder` and `blocking_pick_file` through the plugin, so choosing a project path arms the same race for the rest of the session. `crash/mod.rs:187` also uses the plugin, deliberately: it runs when the main loop may already be gone, which is exactly the case a GTK dialog on the main thread cannot serve. Upstream is no help — rfd 0.17.2 has the same design, and its stop flag is a second `Arc` the thread never sees, so the loop cannot be stopped at all.
  - AC: after choosing a project folder and a project file, `/proc/self/task` holds no thread running `gtk_main_iteration` other than the main one, asserted by a check that fails when the plugin path is restored.
  - AC: the picker still returns a cancelled choice as a cancellation, proved by `pnpm e2e:picker-thread`.
  - **The "opens at the last used directory" clause was struck, 2026-08-31.** It described a behaviour that never existed: nothing here stored a directory, rfd never called `set_current_folder`, and GTK 3.24's `last-folder-uri` is a dead key. The chooser opened on GTK's Recent list before the change and opens on Recent after it. Making the picker remember is new behaviour with its own check, filed as N7 rather than smuggled in under a criterion that read as preservation.

- [x] **N7 The picker does not remember where you were (minor, filed 2026-08-31).** Every project path is chosen from GTK's Recent list, so a translator working through a series picks their way back to the same folder each time. Split out of N1c, whose second criterion asked to preserve a memory that had never been implemented.
  - AC: choosing a folder, closing the app and choosing again opens the chooser at the folder chosen last, proved by a check that fails when the stored path is ignored.
  - AC: a remembered folder that no longer exists opens the chooser at its default rather than failing.
  - AC: nothing is remembered from a cancelled chooser.
  - Status 2026-09-01, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: each of the five chooser kinds remembers its own folder, one per kind rather than one for all — a translator who has just attached a subtitle and then opens a video is not in the same folder. The folders live in `chooser-folders.json` in the app data directory, a convenience and not the user's data, so nothing of this goes near a project folder (decision 20). Only a chosen path is written, so a cancelled chooser leaves the memory as it was, and a remembered folder that is no longer a directory is dropped before the chooser is built. `pnpm e2e:picker-thread` grew from 9 checks to 14: a second run of the app over the same data home opens the folder chooser and accepts it without naming anything, which a `SelectFolder` chooser answers with the folder it is showing. Measured by deleting the `set_current_folder` call and rebuilding: the check stops at that step with exit 1 and the chooser still on screen, because GTK's Recent list has an insensitive accept button, and passes 14/14 with the call restored.

- [x] **N4 Launching without a display panics instead of explaining (minor, filed 2026-08-30).** `sublore` with no `DISPLAY` and no `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` — over SSH, in a container, on a display that went away — dies with a raw library panic from `tao-0.35.3/src/platform_impl/linux/event_loop.rs:217`, `Failed to initialize gtk backend!`, plus a `RUST_BACKTRACE` note. Intelligible to a developer and useless to anyone else, and CONTRIBUTING.md section 6 asks for actionable messages at boundaries. Pre-existing, not introduced by the gate 2 range, and found while reproducing a harness artefact rather than by looking for it.
  - AC: with no display reachable, the app exits non-zero having printed one plain line naming the cause and what to do, and no panic trace.
  - AC: a check proves it, and fails if the message goes back to being a panic.
  - Status 2026-09-01, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: `main.rs` refuses a launch whose `DISPLAY` is unset or empty, printing one line and exiting 1 before GTK is touched. `pnpm e2e:no-display` asserts the status, the line, the absence of a panic trace and the absence of a crash report; it is the only check that runs without an X server, so it has its own CI step rather than going through `e2e-check.sh`, which supplies one. Measured by deleting the guard and rebuilding: the check stops at its first assertion with exit 101 and the tao panic back on stderr, then passes again with the guard restored.
  - Left alone deliberately: a `DISPLAY` that names a server nobody is running still panics inside GTK. Proving it is missing means opening a connection, which is a different guard from reading an environment variable, and this AC is about the launch that has no display at all.

- [x] **M3.6 The third route cannot save a document with no file either (filed 2026-09-01).** B2 gave the toolbar's Save and the close gate's Save a way to write a document that has never had a file: they ask for a path. `adopt_answered`, the route where one transcription replaces another, was not given one, so answering Save there still fails with `noPath`. The replacement does not happen and the old document is kept, which is the safe direction and is what `a_save_that_cannot_be_written_replaces_nothing` pins, but it is now the only route that behaves differently from the other two.
  - AC: transcribing over an unsaved transcription and answering Save raises the same first-save chooser the toolbar raises, writes there, and then takes the new cues.
  - AC: cancelling that chooser writes nothing, replaces nothing, and leaves the cues that were there.
  - AC: E2E drives it, and the test that pins the failed-save path keeps its assertion.
  - Status 2026-09-01, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: the third route asks now. `subtitle_adopt_transcription` intercepts the `noPath` its own Save answer raises and puts up `SubtitleFirstSave`, the same chooser the toolbar's Save and the close gate's Save put up; `adopt_answered_at` writes there and replaces under one lock, so a write that fails still replaces nothing. Dismissing that chooser is a cancellation and not a failure, exactly as it is at the gate: nothing written, nothing replaced, the cues left where they were. A chooser that could not be raised stays a failed save. `a_save_that_cannot_be_written_replaces_nothing` keeps its assertion unchanged and still describes a state that happens: it drives the body, and the body still refuses a save with nowhere to write; what is new is the command layer above it asking where. Two E2E tests added to `asr.spec.js`, `EXPECTED_TESTS` 47 to 49, and three Rust tests over the new body. The 49 E2E tests, the close-gate check and the whole Rust suite are green under Xvfb here; Windows compiles under `cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` and is unverified.

- [x] **M3.7 `asr.spec.js` passes state down a chain of nine tests (filed 2026-09-01).** On CI run 33537893939 the four first-save tests failed together, the first of them on `timed out after 20000ms waiting for a toplevel named "Save the subtitle"`. Its own precondition assertion — the document is dirty — passed, so the document was dirty and **had a file**, which makes Save write in place and the chooser it waits for never appear. Main is green with these tests and they have passed three CI runs, so this is intermittent, and the mechanism above is suspected from the log rather than proved.
  - **2026-09-09: the reading this needed now exists.** The AC "a document with no file is asserted to have none" could not be written because nothing on screen said so. Since N57 the window is named `Untitled - Sublore` for exactly that document, and `e2e/lib/x11.js` reads the name, so each first-save test can assert its own precondition instead of inheriting it.
  - **Closed 2026-09-09, verified on Linux, PR #184.** All three criteria met. Each first-save check asserts what document it is working on before it acts: three that it is `Untitled`, and the one whose point is that Ctrl+S has somewhere to write, that it is the file the check before it gave it. The head of the chain makes its own transcription instead of taking the CPU run's. `asr.spec.js` was run ten times in a row: **10 green, 0 failures**, 34 passing lines a run. Nothing was weakened; the assertions were added, and their teeth were proved by telling the check that needs a file to expect none, which goes red naming what the window actually held.
  - The shape of the problem is not in doubt: each test in that file inherits whatever the eight before it left, and whether the open document has a path by the time the first-save tests run depends on a transcription being adopted several tests earlier.
  - AC: each first-save test establishes the state it needs rather than inheriting it, and asserts that state before acting — a document with no file is asserted to have none.
  - AC: the file is run ten times in a row on Linux with no failure, and the number is recorded.
  - AC: no assertion is weakened; a test that cannot establish its own precondition says so and is restructured, not relaxed.

- [x] **The document is drawn on the video frame (2026-09-03).** Decision 7's first half, landed ahead of M2.6 because the owner found it missing and nothing about timing works without seeing the line on the picture. mpv reads a shadow copy of the open document from the app's own cache directory: the user's subtitle file is never what mpv reads, is never written to produce a preview, and no backup is made for one. Every route that changes what should be on screen ends at one refresh, so opening the document first and opening the video first are the same path and neither is a special case.
  - It asks mpv what it holds rather than remembering: the external track list is read back after every change, so a rewrite re-reads the track already loaded instead of stacking a new one per edit, and the media's own embedded subtitle tracks are never touched.
  - **What the check asserts, and what it cannot.** The harness cannot read the picture, for the reason `video-surface.spec.js` gives about Xvfb and llvmpipe. It asserts mpv's own answer instead, which the app reads back and logs: how many external tracks there are, whether ours is the selected one, whether it is visible, and how many characters long the line at the playhead is. That is one step short of the glyphs. **Nobody has seen the words on the frame with their own eyes; the owner's checklist is where that is answered.**
  - Five tests, and all five were shown to go red. Making every refresh add a track instead of re-reading turns three of them red; writing the shadow but never telling mpv turns four red; making the preview save the user's document turns exactly one red, and it is the one that guards the file. `EXPECTED_TESTS` 121 to 126.
  - Found by reading, after the tests were written and before the commit: `show_subtitles` promised to make its path the one external track and did not remove the others, so changing the document's format while a video stayed loaded would leave the old shadow's track behind. Fixed by dropping every external track that is not the wanted one. **That path has no test**: it needs an ASS document and an SRT one opened in turn against one video, and the fixtures for it do not exist yet.
  - **What the owner has to look at with his own eyes, and why it is a specific thing rather than "check it works".** The check reads mpv's `sub-text` property, and mpv is documented to update that property while leaving a paused still frame unredrawn: mpv issues 12130 (subtitles do not appear when switching tracks while paused) and 11936 (OSD and subtitles not refreshed during a still frame) are both open and both sit exactly in the gap between what the check can see and what the user sees. The player opens every file paused at zero (`src-tauri/src/video/player.rs:339`), so this is the normal case and not an edge one. **Owner checklist item: open a video, pause on a cue, edit that cue's text, and look at the frame without touching anything else. The words on the picture have to change.**
  - Also read on the way, and sidestepped rather than fixed: mpv issue 8231 reports `sub-reload` failing after a `sub-remove` of the same track. The code never does that pair, because a document that was dropped comes back through `sub-add`, not through a reload. It does do `sub-remove` of a stale track followed by `sub-reload` of a different one, in the same call, when the document changes format. That sequence has no test either, for the same reason the format switch has none.
  - Also unfixed, and small: the character count at the playhead reads `sub-text` and treats every failure as "no line", so an mpv error there would read as absence. It weakens the instrument, it does not weaken the feature.

- [x] **M2.4 W10 The waveform's two numbers, measured (2026-09-03).** `pnpm e2e:waveform-budget` opens a fixture twice against one data home and reads the app's own account of both runs: when it first handed peaks over, and how long the job took altogether. The first number is guarded in CI on every push; the second belongs to the owner's machine, because a runner is not the machine CONTRIBUTING.md section 7 names and a 24-minute fixture is not a thing to generate on every push.
  - **Measured on the owner's machine (AMD Ryzen 7 7700, 16 threads, Linux), 2026-09-03.** The 60 s fixture: first peaks 171 ms cold and 54 ms warm, read all the way in 355 ms cold and 72 ms warm. The 24-minute fixture: first peaks 162 ms cold and 79 ms warm, read all the way in **4436 ms** cold and 512 ms warm. The budgets are 2000 ms and 20000 ms. Verified on Linux.
  - The stopwatch stops where the peaks are handed over, not where they are produced. The first version stopped at production, and a build that buffered every chunk and delivered them all at the end still passed it, which is the one regression this budget exists to catch. With the stopwatch moved, that same build reports its first peaks and its finish at the same millisecond and the check fails.
  - It also answers the risk the cache wiring left open: replaying a 24-minute file hands over about 1440 chunks with nothing pacing them, and that replay takes 512 ms altogether. It is not a problem.

- [x] **N14 The waveform panel sometimes never appears in CI, and the timeout says nothing about why (filed 2026-09-03).** `waveform.spec.js` fails on `timed out after 30000ms waiting for the waveform panel to appear and hold ink`, and the test after it then runs out mocha's own clock. The panel is gated on the first peak arriving, so that one sentence covers a job that never started, a job that failed, a video that never opened and a canvas that is empty, and it names none of them.
  - Not a spec that has never worked: green in CI on #59 at 3.2s, and green here 3 runs out of 3 at about 3.4s each.
  - Not the D-Bus portal. The first red run was the one that took the portal away, which made it the obvious suspect, and #63 refutes it: portal present, 12m02s of smoke, waveform red all the same.
  - Not the fixtures. `make-waveform-fixtures.sh` runs in the e2e job and the job log holds all three files with their probed streams; `requireWaveformFixture()` would refuse the run outright if one were missing, and the spec's first test passes.
  - Not concurrency between runners: `maxInstances` is 1, so the fourteen spec files run one after another in the green run and in the red ones alike.
  - Not the fourteenth spec on a warm machine either: the whole suite in order here is 14 files, 93 tests, green, with the waveform 4 for 4 at 3.2s in its usual last position.
  - **What the diagnostic says, run 33704913953.** `the video is open, 0 of 60s; no .waveform__canvas (panel absent); no failure is on the status bar.` So mpv has the file and knows its length, the panel never appeared, and no `audio://error` ever arrived. The job produced neither peaks nor a failure, which leaves the two branches in `start_for_playing_track` that return before `start_job` and tell only the log: mpv refusing to list the tracks, and no track in the list carrying `selected`.
  - **Reproduced 2026-09-03, and it is the second branch.** `playing` is mpv's `track-list/N/selected`, read on the thread that has just loaded the file, and W5 had already found the same shape one field over: asking mpv for the path right after a successful open answered `None` on a loaded runner, and that branch returned in silence too. Forcing `selected` to `false` in `player.rs` and running the spec against the code as it was reproduces CI down to its shape: 2 passing, 2 failing, 1m 33s, the same diagnostic sentence, and the app's own log saying `has no playing audio track, so nothing is peaked; mpv listed 1 audio tracks: stream 1 selected=false`. With the same forcing and `track_to_peak` in place: 4 passing in 3.3s, and the log says it fell back and to which stream.
  - So it is a product defect and not a test one. On a machine slow enough, a video that carries audio opened with no waveform at all, and the only thing that said so was a log line CI was throwing away.
  - Closed by `track_to_peak`: the track mpv is playing, or the first audio track when mpv has marked none. A file with no audio still spawns no child, which is decision 24 E3 and is the empty list rather than the fallback.
  - Left open on purpose: **why** mpv has not marked a track by `file-loaded` on a loaded runner. The fallback makes the panel right either way and says out loud when it was needed, so the runs still carry the evidence. AC for closing this entry: that line stops appearing in CI logs, or its cause is named.
  - **Read again 2026-09-09, and the user-visible half is already closed.** `track_to_peak` in `src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs` falls back to the first track when mpv has marked none as playing, so the panel draws either way and the symptom this entry opens with cannot happen. What is still unmet is the entry's own narrower criterion: `start_for_playing_track` still logs that mpv marked no track, and nobody has named why mpv fails to mark one on a loaded runner. So this stays open on that point alone, which is smaller than the entry reads.
  - **Closed 2026-09-09: the cause is named, which is what this entry's own AC asked for.** mpv defines `track-list/N/selected` as "yes/true if the track is currently **decoded**", and `file-loaded` as happening "after a file was loaded and begins playback" (mpv v0.41.0's manual). So the read asks whether decoding has begun and the code was treating the answer as which track mpv had chosen. On a loaded machine decoding trails the event and every track reads false. **It is mpv answering the question it was asked**, not mpv being slow to choose, so there is nothing in mpv to wait for and nothing to fix. `track_to_peak`'s fallback is the right shape rather than a workaround, and its comment and the log line now say so (PR #188).
- [x] **N21 The timing keyboard is the numeric keypad, and nothing here can dispatch it yet (filed 2026-09-04, CLOSED 2026-09-04).** Read off the reference's own default bindings rather than guessed: commit is KP_Enter, play selection KP_5, play before KP_1, play after KP_3, stop KP_8, shorten KP_7. The whole timing workflow sits on the numpad, one hand, no modifiers. That is the layout the work is done in and it is worth having.
  - **Sublore cannot dispatch it today, and the reason is not the parser.** `acceleratorFor` now reads the registry, but it requires Ctrl, and a bare key needs to know first whether it belongs to a text field or to the shell. That question is answered in `CueList.tsx` by `ownsTheKeyboard`, which also owns the flush of a pending cue editor. A modifier-free layer means moving both, or giving the shell its own copy, and a second copy of that rule is how the two drift.
  - **Also unresolved:** a numpad key arrives as `event.code` (`Numpad1`), not as `event.key`, because `event.key` is `"1"` or `"End"` depending on Num Lock. Any binding table has to say which of the two it matches on, and the accelerator strings today carry neither.
  - **Until it exists** the four nudge commands and the four playback commands are menu items with no key, which is the slow way to do the fast part of the job. They work; they are not yet worth using.
  - **What it needs:** one keyboard context that both the shell and the grid read, holding the flush and the text-field rule once. That is a task of its own and it is the one that makes every timing command actually usable.
  - **CLOSED, and the entry above was wrong about what the task was.** The keypad is not the window context's to dispatch: interface-spec 10.9 already refuses the always-on numpad layer, on the grounds that every command in it has a waveform-context binding anyway. What the window context did owe, and had owed since the spec was written, is 10.4's Ctrl+digit bindings. Four of those name commands that exist, and they landed. The keypad now belongs to the waveform context, which needs a focused-panel layer that does not exist yet.
  - **What actually moved.** One listener, in `src/keyboard.ts`, read by the shell. `CueList.tsx` lost its own window listener, three props and the field rule, because the reason it held them was wrong: every command in `App.tsx` already flushed both editors, so the grid's copy was a narrower flush in front of a proper one, plus a route around the availability gate. Ctrl+Z, Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+S now run through the gate a click runs through, which is the last route that did not.
  - **Measured, not assumed, before any of it was designed.** A keypad key's `code` is stable across Num Lock and its `key` is not. `Ctrl+Shift+1` delivers `key: "!"`, so a digit cannot be matched on `key` at all. Both readings are in docs/keyboard-tasks.md with the probe's numbers.
  - **A blocker for whoever builds the waveform context, found here and worth having early:** interface-spec 10.6 gives it `Numpad +` and `Numpad −`, and under xdotool on this keymap both arrive with `altKey: true`, preceded by a real `AltLeft` press, in both Num Lock states. Any dispatcher that excludes Alt will never see them, so those two bindings cannot be checked through this harness as it stands. `Numpad5` and `NumpadEnter` arrive clean.

  - **One code path of this is closed (2026-09-05).** `waveform-tracks.spec.js` "draws the quarter-scale track after switching to it" went red on the runner with the menu marking track one while the panel drew track two. Not a race and not a regression: the code was byte identical across a passing run and a failing one. The two answers were computed in two places from two different rules. The panel drew the stream chosen by the requested id (`audio/mod.rs:358`), and the mark came from mpv's own `selected` flag read one statement after the write, folded in the page as `tracks.find((t) => t.playing) ?? tracks[0]` (`useAudioTracks.ts:72`). Where mpv marks nothing, both stale readings land on the first track.
  - **The fix gives the question one owner.** Both audio commands now answer `AudioTrackList { tracks, currentId }`, where `currentId` is the track the panel is drawing: `track_to_peak`'s choice on the open path, the id that was asked for on the switch path. `AudioTrack.playing` still means mpv's flag and nothing else, so decision 24 E2 is not overruled, and a switch mpv has not marked now writes a log line naming every stream and what mpv said about it. The open question this entry exists for, why mpv marks no track on a loaded runner, is untouched.
  - **The owner call this leaves.** E2 says mpv is the authority on which track is playing. Answering the switch from the id that was requested is the app overruling mpv on that one path, and no written rule says whether E2 governs only the open path, where the app has no other information, or the switch path too, where the app is the one that chose.
- [x] **N31 The window tree walk cannot survive the thing the shutdown check exists to cause (filed and fixed 2026-09-05).** `shutdown` failed on CI with `X Error: 9: Bad Drawable`, `X Error: 3: Bad Window` and `xwininfo: error: Can't query window tree`. `xwininfo -root -tree` lists the children and then asks about each, so a window destroyed between the two ends the whole walk, and the check doing the destroying is the one that broke. `describeOrNull` in the same file already forgives exactly this one window at a time, with a comment saying it cost a CI run's diagnosis once before: the lesson was written down and applied one function short of where it was needed, which is the shape `closeAnyOpenProject` had the same night. `rootTree` now reads again, up to three times, and still throws for anything that is not the race. Green on Linux, where the race does not reproduce, so what is proved here is that nothing was broken by the change.

- [ ] **N30 A refused open puts the file's text back in the grid while the session still holds the edit (filed 2026-09-05).** `subtitle.spec.js`'s "throws an unsaved edit away and writes nothing when the edit is discarded" failed on CI for `8094575` at line 273: the row was expected to still read "Typed and then thrown away" and read "The harbour was empty when we got there.", which is the file's own first cue. It passes on Linux in every run tonight, so it is a runner-only sighting so far.
  - **The probe this asked for landed 2026-09-09, PR #184, and the entry stays open.** The discard check now reads the row and what the session says it is holding as one assertion, at the moment of the refusal. It needed nothing added to the app: since N57 the window carries the document's name and the unsaved mark, so a grid showing the file's own text while the window still says the session holds an edit is now a failure that names which of the two disagreed. Open until the sighting comes back and is read, or until it is old enough to call a loaded runner.
  - **The backend did the right thing, and the app's own log says so.** `chooser: chose a subtitle: .../discard-basic-lf.srt` appears twice, and `subtitle: opened .../discard-basic-lf.srt` appears once, after the first. The second open wrote no `opened` line: it was refused, which is the 2026-09-03 ruling working. The discard button came alive, which the check waited for and got.
  - **So what disagreed is the grid.** Between the edit landing, which the check waited for and asserted dirty on, and the read three assertions later, the row went back to the file's text while nothing reopened the file. If that is what it looks like, it is user visible and it is not a harness fault: a refused open would be showing the user their edit vanishing while the session still holds it.
  - **What it needs.** The two readings taken together at the moment of the refusal, the row and the session's own revision, which needs a probe this check does not have. Not attempted: it was found at the end of a long night and filing it beat guessing at it.
  - **It has not come back, and the run after it says why that matters.** The next CI run was documentation only, so every executable byte was the same, and it failed on a different check again, `shutdown`, for a reason of its own (below). Three runs, three unrelated checks, one unchanged Linux build: the battery itself passed on the third. So this entry may be one sighting of a loaded runner rather than a defect, and the way to tell them apart is whether it ever comes back.

- [x] **N29 `check (windows-latest)` fails to load the library's own test binary, and the diagnostic looks at the wrong file (filed 2026-09-05, closed the same day).** Green on `8094575` with the manifest dependency in `.cargo/config.toml`. `cargo test --workspace` on Windows dies with `sublore_lib-<hash>.exe (exit code: 0xc0000139, STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND)`, before a single test runs. Green on `1a48034`, red on `1ec009e` and again on `db8dfe9`, so it is reproducible and H8 is what changed. Windows compiling is a shipping condition of this project and this is a real break, not a deferral.
  - **The diagnostic named a symbol and it is the wrong one.** `.github/scripts/windows-entrypoint.ps1` reported `comctl32.dll : MISSING TaskDialogIndirect`, under `video_playback-<hash>.exe`. That is not the binary that died, and the script never looked at the one that did: it inspected two hardcoded files. `TaskDialogIndirect` lives in comctl32 version 6, which a binary gets from an application manifest and a Rust test binary has none, so every manifest-less binary here would report it. The script's own header warns about exactly this class of false positive. It now inspects every `target\debug\deps\*.exe`, so the next run names the binary that failed. That change is written and has never been run: there is no PowerShell on the owner's machine.
  - **What was ruled out from Linux, by reading and by measuring.** `Cargo.lock` has not moved since the module work, so it is not a dependency. H8 adds no manifest entry, no dev-dependency, and no `use` beyond `AtomicBool`, `AtomicU64`, `Arc`, `Mutex`, `AppHandle`, `Emitter` and `Manager`, all of them already linked by this crate. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -- -D warnings` is clean, so it compiles. Linking the Windows test binary here to read its import table was attempted and refused: `ld: cannot find -lmpv`, because libmpv for Windows is on the runner and not on this machine.
  - **The widened diagnostic ran, and it refutes the symbol it named.** On `5998e81` it inspected 51 binaries and found `comctl32!TaskDialogIndirect` missing from five of them: `crash_main_thread`, `crash_safety`, `sublore.exe`, `sublore_lib` and `video_playback`. Four of those load and run; one does not. A symbol four binaries import without dying is not what kills the fifth. Its closing sentence is the finding: every named import of every binary resolves through the loader, so what is missing is reached by ordinal or lies a DLL further down the graph.
  - **The local comparison was made and it refutes H8 as the cause too.** The pinned libmpv archive was fetched to a scratch directory, its checksum matched the one `install-libmpv-windows.ps1` holds, and the Windows test binary was linked here for the gnu target at HEAD and at `1a48034`, the last commit whose Windows job was green. **The two import tables are identical: 450 entries each, no addition and no removal**, and both already contain `TaskDialogIndirect`. H8 adds no dependency, no manifest entry, and no reference to the dialog or the video from the module code. What that experiment cannot separate is the linker: the gnu linker keeps the whole graph and the MSVC linker on the runner keeps only what is referenced, so a change in reachability would not show here.
  - **It did not stop there, and the answer is `WebView2Loader.dll`.** The trace was taken on the owner's own machine after all: wine 11.0 is installed, the mingw target links against the pinned libmpv's own `.dll.a`, and `WINEDEBUG=+loaddll` prints the loader's decisions. Running the Windows test binary under it gives `err:module:import_dll Library WebView2Loader.dll (which is needed by sublore_lib-<hash>.exe) not found`. `webview2-com-sys` puts that DLL in `target\debug`, the test binaries live in `target\debug\deps`, and Windows searches the executable's own directory first: the library's test binary is the one that links the webview, so it is the one that goes looking. Here nothing answers and the status is `c0000135`, DLL not found; on the runner something answers and the status is `c0000139`, the entry point is missing, which is the same miss one step further along.
  - **Proved by running it, which is what this entry was missing.** With `WebView2Loader.dll` copied beside the binary, the same binary starts and lists its 157 tests. Without it, it will not start. The CI step that already puts libmpv beside the test binaries now puts this one there too, and throws rather than copying nothing if the build script has not produced it.
  - **The first copy shipped the wrong architecture, and the runner said so in bytes.** `webview2-com-sys` ships `arm64`, `x64` and `x86` beside each other, a recursive first match takes `arm64`, and the step reported placing a 145984 byte library where the x64 one is 160320. Measured here rather than argued: with the arm64 library beside the x64 binary wine answers `c000007b`, an invalid image format, and the process does not start; with the x64 one it starts and lists 156 tests. The step now filters on the `x64` directory instead of taking the first match. The runner's own status differed, `c0000139` rather than `c000007b`, which says it rejected the arm64 library and then found another WebView2Loader that answers to the name and not to the imports.
  - **The runner refuted it, and the copy has been taken back out.** With the x64 library placed beside the test binaries, 160320 bytes, the binary still dies the same way. The widened diagnostic then said why the whole line of thought was wrong: the MSVC `sublore_lib` test binary imports 22 DLLs and **neither `WebView2Loader.dll` nor `libmpv-2.dll` is among them**. The gnu build imports both and the MSVC build imports neither, so the wine trace named a real gap in a binary that is not the one CI runs. The step is back to libmpv alone rather than carrying a copy nothing needs and a `throw` that could break the job for a reason of its own.
  - **`TaskDialogIndirect` is refuted by the runner too, and this time by what ran.** `crash_main_thread`, `crash_safety` and `video_playback` all import it, and all three start and pass in the same job: one passing test binary would be enough, and there are three. The diagnostic resolves symbols from its own PowerShell process, which has comctl32 version 5, so it reports that symbol missing for every manifest-less binary. It is the false positive its own header warns about, and the header should say this one out loud.
  - **The lists are identical, which is the last thing this instrument can say.** The DLL sets of the binary that dies and of the three that run differ by nothing: no DLL the dead one imports is absent from the live ones, and none of theirs is missing from it. So the difference is a symbol and not a library, every named symbol resolves, and no DLL is imported by ordinal. What is left is one level down: a DLL among those 22 that itself cannot find something. Naming it needs the diagnostic to walk the second level, or a loader trace on Windows.
  - **It is H8 and not the runner, settled by re-running the green commit.** `1a48034`'s Windows job was re-run on 2026-09-05, hours after the failures began, on whatever runner image is current: it passes. The same code that was green is green today, so the image did not move under it and the difference is the one commit between them. That is the measurement that should have been taken first, and it was taken fifth.
  - **Named, by diffing the import tables of the green build and the red one.** The Windows job was made to dump `sublore_lib`'s own table whether it passed or failed, and the same binary was built once with H8's Rust reverted and once with it. Green: **235 named imports**. Red: **402**. The 167 in the difference are the whole windowing stack, `user32` (`CreateWindowExW`, `RegisterClassExW`, `SendInput`, `GetTouchInputInfo`), `gdi32`, `dwmapi`, `ole32` (`OleInitialize`, `RegisterDragDrop`), `shell32`, and `comctl32` with `SetWindowSubclass` and `TaskDialogIndirect`. So H8 makes tao and wry reachable from the library's test binary, and `TaskDialogIndirect` arrives with them.
  - **`TaskDialogIndirect` was the cause after all, and the refutation above was mine and wrong.** It read "three binaries import it and run", which took the diagnostic's word about those three instead of reading their own tables, from an instrument that had just been shown to answer about its own process. The unreferenced code being dropped is also what the 235 against 402 proves, so the run with the six slots at `None` did mean what it appeared to and the callbacks are still not the entry point: what pulls the window in is elsewhere in H8's wiring, and that is still open.
  - **The fix is the manifest, and it is the same shape as the libmpv one.** `.cargo/config.toml` gives every Windows binary the `Microsoft.Windows.Common-Controls` version 6 dependency the app already declares through Tauri, so a test binary loads the comctl32 the product loads. It is not a narrowing of the check: the check was failing on a library the product never runs without.
  - **Still open, and worth its own look:** which line of H8 makes the windowing stack reachable from a test binary at all. Nothing in it names a window, and a test binary that links tao is a test binary carrying code it never runs.
  - **Where it stands, and the decision that is the owner's.** Five attempts, each refuted by the runner rather than by argument, and the mechanism is not named. CONTRIBUTING.md rules that Windows *compiles* and that no Windows behaviour is claimed until the activation milestone; this job runs `cargo test --workspace`, which is behaviour, and what is red is a test binary that will not load in a harness the product never ships in. Narrowing the job to a compile would be green in a minute and is exactly the weakening this repository refuses, so it has not been done and will not be without a ruling. The job stays red and says why.
  - **An owner decision sits underneath it, and it is not one to take quietly.** CONTRIBUTING.md rules that Windows *compiles* on every push and that no Windows behaviour is claimed until the activation milestone. This job runs `cargo test --workspace`, which is behaviour, and what is red is a test binary that will not load in a harness the product never ships in: the app itself carries a manifest and the test binary does not. Narrowing the job to a compile would turn it green in a minute and **that is exactly the kind of weakening this repository refuses**, so it has not been done. Whether the Windows job should compile or should run tests is the owner's, and until he says, the job stays red and says so.

- [x] **N35 A machine with no sound could not open a media that is only audio (filed 2026-09-06, closed 2026-09-06).** `video-aspect.spec.js`'s no-picture check failed on CI four runs out of four and never here. The diagnostic the check now prints settled it: the window is still there, and the application's own log reads `chooser: chose a video: picture-none.mkv`, then `video: this media carries no picture, so there is no size to report`, then `ERROR player error PlaybackStopped: mpv error -14 (playback)`. Error -14 is `MPV_ERROR_AO_INIT_FAILED`, read off `libmpv2-sys`'s bindings rather than guessed: the audio output would not initialise. A runner has no sound card, and for a media that is only audio the audio output is the whole of the playback, so mpv stops and the event loop turned that into a user-facing `PlaybackStopped`. Every other fixture carries video, which is why nothing else saw it. **The file had already loaded when it happened**, which is what makes the fix small and the behaviour clear: a machine with no sound is still a machine a media can be open on, and an editor of subtitles wants the file and its waveform whether or not the speakers exist. `player.rs` now says it once in the log and tells the user nothing. A failure before the open resolves is still an open that failed, and still reaches the user.

- [ ] **N38 The current line's box keeps its pixels while its contents scale, so a large interface loses a control off the bottom (filed 2026-09-06).** The top block opens at a pixel count and an interface size change deliberately does not move it: `interface-scale.spec.js`'s "leaves the three panels at the proportions the sashes were left at when the size changes" asserts `bigger.block === left.block`, and its reason is the waveform, whose measurements are in device pixels because a peak bucket is one millisecond. Everything inside the panel is in rem and does scale. So at 150 per cent in the narrowest window the panel is 1.5 times fuller in a box that has not moved, and the text box needs the panel's scroll to be reached. It has been paid for three times by raising the block's opening height, from 13.5rem to 21rem as the panel gained the effect, the drawing order, the three margins, Next line and the style dropdown, and raising it cannot fix the 150 per cent case because the shortfall there grows with the size. The two candidates are scaling the stored heights with the interface size, which contradicts that criterion and needs the owner, and the reference's own answer, which is merging and splitting the panel's rows by width (edit-bar-tasks.md question 1).

- [x] **N39 The spectrum the colour picker does not have (filed 2026-09-06; the font half closed 2026-09-07, the alpha half the same day; the spectrum 2026-09-09).** The font button is in, over a font enumeration that reads the `name` table out of every installed font file, and over a planner that writes several tags as one step, which is what this entry said both halves wanted. The transparency went in on that same planner: the picker takes a number from 0 to 255 and writes `\1a` to `\4a` beside the colour, as one undo step, and an empty field writes none at all, which is this product's answer to the reference writing it only when it changed. What is left is the spectrum, where the picker offers sixteen colours and a field that takes `#RRGGBB` and the reference offers a saturation square, a hue slider, the three number notations and a screen eyedropper. What it said when it was filed: Row three names nine buttons before Next line: four style flags, a font, and four colours. The flags and the colours are built; the font is not, and it is the one that needs something this repository does not have, a list of the fonts installed on the machine. Beside it two halves of the colour picker are missing. The alpha: a colour is written with its transparency beside it, `\1a` to `\4a`, and only when that transparency changed, which is two tags in one undo step and `Edit::SetOverrideTag` writes one. The spectrum: the picker offers sixteen colours and a field that takes `#RRGGBB`, where a saturation square, a hue slider, the three number notations and a screen eyedropper belong. The alpha and the font want the same thing first, a planner that writes a list of tags as one step.

  - **Closed 2026-09-09, verified on Linux.** The square, the hue slider, the preview and four notations that are one state drawn five ways. The picker holds HSV rather than deriving it, because a grey has no hue and a derived slider would swing to red the moment white was picked; that is the case the check pins and the mutation proves. **Two things stay out and are now their own work: N53 the five spectrum modes, and N54 the eyedropper.**
- [x] **N42 The style editor holds ten of a style's twenty-three columns, because the parser reads ten (filed 2026-09-07, closed the same day).** The parser reads all twenty-three now, the editor draws them in the groups the reference's own dialog uses, and the alignment is the grid rather than a number: nine buttons where ASS counts from the bottom left, so the top row is 7, 8, 9. The opaque box is drawn as what it is, a border style writing 3 or 1, and not as a fifth flag. What it said when it was filed: Edit beside the Style dropdown opens on the font, the size, the four colours and the four flags, and writes each as its own undo step over the span the parser recorded. What it does not hold is everything the parser never read: the outline and shadow widths, the border style, the three margins, the alignment, the rotation and scaling, the spacing and the encoding. They are not drawn as empty boxes, because a box that cannot be written is worse than an absence; adding them is parser work in `ass.rs` and `AssStyle` before it is dialog work, and the dialog then grows by a line each. The style's **name** is a separate matter and not this entry's: renaming one means rewriting every event that names it, which is a different operation with its own undo step.

- [x] **N43 Cut and paste are not built, because both want an edit that adds or removes several cues as one step (filed 2026-09-07, closed the same day).** Closed by five slices that all needed the same thing and got it: `DeleteMany` takes a whole selection out in one splice, `Paste` puts the clipboard's own lines back in, `Duplicate` writes each run again after itself, `Join` folds two or more into the first, and Delete now takes the selection rather than the row the cursor is on. What a paste lands is the fragment itself rather than a rendering of it, read behind this document's own header first, so every field a copy carried comes back with the line. **What it said when it was filed:** copy, paste over and select all were in, over a clipboard that goes through GTK because the page cannot reach one, and the other half of the group was missing because `Edit::Delete` and `Edit::Insert` each name one cue, so two cues cut would have been two undo steps. The narrower paste over is not closed here and is now N45.

- [x] **N45 Paste over takes the text and keeps everything else, where the reference asks which fields to take (filed 2026-09-07, closed 2026-09-09).** The reference opens a dialog on the first paste over of a session, with a checked list of the eleven fields an event has, and reuses the answer for every row that paste touches. Sublore's takes the text and leaves the times, the style, the speaker, the effect and the three margins as they were, which is the common case and is what the check pins. Closing this is the dialog, the eleven checkboxes, and the answer being remembered for the rest of the session; the planner already writes whole rows, so it is UI work rather than edit work. Filed out of N43 when cut and paste closed it, so the half of that entry that is still true does not close with the half that is not.

  - **Closed 2026-09-09, verified on Linux.** The entry had two things wrong, both found by reading the reference rather than remembering it. It is not UI work: for a paste to stay one undo step the planner needed a plural field write it did not have, so this added `Edit::PasteOverCues` to `sublore-edit`. And the dialog does not open once a session: the vector of answers there is a local inside the command, so it opens on every paste over and only the boxes it opens with are remembered. What was built is the reference's behaviour, not the entry's.
- [ ] **N46 The waveform's strip can want more rows than its panel holds at the narrowest window, and the panel's floor is a number that does not know what it draws (filed 2026-09-07).** `MIN_WAVEFORM_HEIGHT` is 64 at 100 per cent and the strip is fourteen words that wrap; how many rows they wrap onto is what the machine's fonts decide. On this repository's runner every button is on screen at the floor; on the CI runner, at 110 per cent, the last one is behind the strip's own scroll. The check now pins that one button as a ceiling and asserts the scroll reaches it, which is honest and is not a fix. **This is the third panel with the same defect** and the first two were closed the day this was filed: the tools column's floor did not know the width its current line needs, and the block's floor read the edge between two panels as what was left over rather than as itself. Both are measured now. The wave panel's height floor is the one left, and it is harder: the strip's wrapped height depends on the panel's width, so a floor read off it feeds back into the layout that produced it. The cheap half is worth doing first, which is deciding whether the panel may take height from the grid at all, because that is an owner question and not an engineering one.

- [ ] **N44 The clipboard is not implemented on Windows (filed 2026-09-07).** `src-tauri/src/clipboard.rs` is GTK on Linux and a stub that returns an error everywhere else. That is deliberate under the 2026-09-04 ruling, which says the behavioural suite is Linux only and no Windows behaviour is claimed, and a stub that returned success would be a claim. It compiles on the Windows target and always will, because the stub has no platform dependency at all. Closing it is Windows work and belongs with MW.

- [x] **N47 The menus draw no separators, and the reference groups every menu with them (filed 2026-09-08).** `MenuEntry` in `src/types/chrome.ts` is `CommandId | Submenu` with no separator kind, so no Sublore menu draws the rules the reference uses to group items: File, Edit, Timing and Help all show separated groups in `default_menu.json` and Sublore shows one flat list. Closing it is a separator variant of `MenuEntry`, its rendering in `MenuBar.tsx`, the arrow walk stepping over it the way it steps over a disabled item, and the separators added to each menu's `items` in the order interface-spec §3 records. Cross-cutting on purpose: it touches every menu at once, which is why the Help menu slice drew its six items flat and filed this rather than adding separators to one menu.
- [x] **N48 The event-log window is not built, so Help's Event log stays greyed (filed 2026-09-08, closed 2026-09-09).** `help.event-log` is drawn greyed. Interface-spec §9.12: a window listing recent internal log messages, worth having because the owner verifies by behaviour and "open the log window and read it back to me" is a step a non-coder can follow. The app already logs through tauri-plugin-log to a file; the window reads that back. Closing it ungreys the command.
