This entry stays open as an owner look rather than as work waiting to start, and nothing is blocked behind it: M2.4 closed, M2.6 closed, and M2.7 closed while it stood.
- [x] **M2.7 The cue structure edits reach the interface (filed 2026-09-01, owner ruling).** `lib.rs` registers `subtitle_insert`, `subtitle_delete`, `subtitle_split`, `subtitle_merge` and `subtitle_set_times`, and no frontend file invokes any of the five. M2.1 built the mutation API and M2.3 gave text editing a route; these five have no gesture, no menu item and no button, so a user cannot split a cue, merge two, insert one, delete one, or change a timing without editing the file by hand. Filed as its own task rather than folded into M2.5, because the backend is done and what is missing is only the route.
  - AC: from the interface alone, with a subtitle open, a user can insert a cue, delete one, split one at the cursor, merge two adjacent ones, and change a cue's start and end. Each lands in the document and is visible in the grid.
  - AC: each of the five is undoable with the same undo the text edits use, and redoable, and the file on disk is unchanged until a save.
  - AC: E2E drives all five through the interface and asserts the saved file afterwards; a test that calls the command directly does not satisfy this criterion, because the hole being closed is the route, not the command.
  - AC: inserting or deleting a cue moves the cursor and the selection with the rows, so neither points at a different line than it did before, and neither is left past the end. T6 left the two states indexed by row and nothing in the interface changes the row count yet, so the remap is owed here rather than built ahead of a caller.

- [x] **M2.1 Editable document model.** Mutation API over `sublore-formats` (edit cue text, edit times, insert, delete, split, merge) that keeps the lossless guarantee: everything the parser preserved stays preserved for untouched cues, and every mutation re-runs the tiling/coverage guard M1 added.
  - AC: mutating one cue in a fixture and saving leaves every other byte of the file identical; a mutation that would break segment coverage is refused with a structured error, never written; property test over random edit sequences never produces a document that fails the guard.
- [x] **M2.2 Undo/redo.** Single undo stack for every document mutation, with coalescing of consecutive typing into one entry.
  - AC: any sequence of edits can be undone back to the exact original bytes and redone forward to the exact edited bytes; undo depth is bounded and documented; typing a word is one undo step, not one per character.
- [x] **M2.3 Cue list UI with editing.** Virtualized cue list (index, start, end, text), inline text editing, keyboard navigation, dirty state, save/save-as.
  - AC: E2E: open the 2000-cue fixture, edit a cue's text, save, reopen, the edit is there and the rest is byte-identical; undo restores it; scrolling and typing show no visible lag (measured, budget CONTRIBUTING.md §7: open under 1 s).
  - **Status 2026-09-02:** the five tests carrying this milestone's own acceptance sentence lived behind the `real-media` cargo feature, and CI enabled it nowhere and generated the fixtures nowhere, so from W2 landing until today they had never run there. `cargo test` on this crate was green and was not saying what it looked like it was saying. They run on Linux on every push now, with a guard that fails the step if the named test is no longer in the suite, because a feature that stops being passed compiles the file away and leaves a green step behind.

- [x] **M2.4 Waveform.** Audio peaks extracted from the media (via the existing libmpv/ffmpeg path, off the main thread, cancellable) and rendered as a zoomable waveform with the playhead.
  - AC: peaks for a 60 s fixture appear within budget and match the audio (silence reads flat, the 440 Hz tone reads full); playhead tracks playback; zoom and scroll stay responsive; no main-thread blocking.
  - AC (decision 12, audio provider): reuse the ASR *pattern* — ffmpeg discovery, background execution, progress, cancellation — and not its code. Extraction runs at full quality behind a public API with a per-episode cache, and its lifetime is tied to the episode, not to a transcription run. The ASR extractor is private, produces mono 16 kHz because whisper wants that (`sublore-asr/src/sidecar.rs:285,302-304`), and writes into a scratch folder that deletes itself when the run ends (`scratch.rs:88-91`): fine for peaks, wrong for playing a selection, and it would vanish mid-session.
  - **Closed 2026-09-03, verified on Linux, ten tasks of ten (W1 to W10).** The panel draws peaks the audio crate produces off the main thread, the sash stores its height, zoom and follow keep the playhead on screen, the Audio menu lists the tracks and switching redraws, a media file with no audio says so instead of drawing nothing, and the peaks cache is wired to the thing that reads it. Both numbers CONTRIBUTING.md section 7 claims are measured by a check that can fail: first peaks under two seconds is guarded in CI on every push, and the 24-minute read is the owner's machine's (4436 ms cold, 512 ms warm). Windows compiles and has run none of it.
- [ ] **M2.5 Timing against the waveform.** Drag cue boundaries on the waveform, nudge with keyboard, snap to playhead; changes flow through the M2.1 mutation API and undo stack.
  - AC: E2E: drag a cue boundary, the model times change accordingly and save round-trips; nudge shortcuts move by the documented step; every timing change is undoable.
  - AC (filed 2026-08-29): the keyboard command set is what makes timing fast, and dragging alone does not cover it. Previous/next line, play selection, play line, play before/after/begin/end of selection, play to end, lead in, lead out, and commit each have a shortcut and each is exercised by a behavioural test.
  - AC (decision 4, one undo per operation): `sublore-edit` grows a composite history entry — a transaction of N child edits under a single label. Today an entry carries one `Splice` (`history.rs:37-39`), `Edit` names a single cue (`plan.rs:28-58`), and coalescing needs matching label and offset (`history.rs:192-195`), so edits on different cues never merge. Test: edit scattered rows, one undo returns the document byte-identical. Range variants were rejected because sparse selections, not contiguous ranges, are the real case.
  - **First slice done 2026-09-04, verified on Linux: dragging a boundary.** The cursor's cue puts two markers on the waveform and either can be grabbed, eight device pixels wide converted to milliseconds at the current zoom, taking the nearer of the two. What the document is told is the release and never the travel, through `subtitle_set_times`, so a whole drag is one undo step. A release that would put the end at or before the start is refused rather than clamped, because nothing below clamps it either. Six behavioural checks.
  - Note (decision 11): the product reasons in **milliseconds**, final. No frame, framerate or keyframe vocabulary, and no timing item carries a "pending a frame engine" reservation. If a frame seam is ever needed it lives in the player and nowhere else.
  - **Status 2026-09-09.** Everything here is built except decision 4's own shape. Dragging landed 2026-09-04; the keyboard set landed since, and Play line, Play selection, Play before and Play after are commands with keys and strip buttons. What is not built is the composite history entry: `Entry` in `crates/sublore-edit/src/history.rs` still carries one `Splice`. The behaviour that criterion exists for is met by another route, because the multi-cue edits N43 closed each move a whole run in a single splice and so are each one undo step. So the question left is whether the composite shape is still wanted for M7's use of it, which is an owner call and not engineering left undone.
  - **Status 2026-09-11, and it narrows further.** N148 gave the field and comment writes the same shape the multi-cue text and time edits already had: `ascending_run` locates the run a list of cues spans and `splice_over` builds one splice across it, so a write over scattered rows is one entry and one undo step. A unit test writes cues 0 and 2, leaves cue 1 byte-identical, and takes all of it back with one undo, which is decision 4's own criterion read literally. `Entry` still carries one `Splice`, so the composite type is still not there; what is there is every behaviour the criterion asked for. The owner call is unchanged and smaller than it was.
- [x] **M2.6 Source/target side by side.** Two documents open at once (source and target), aligned by index, editing only the target.
  - AC: open two fixtures as source and target; rows align; editing the target never mutates the source file on disk; saving writes only the target.
  - AC (decision 13, how a translation is born): a **New translation from source** command creates a document inheriting the source's cues and timings with empty text; the source is read-only while translating; the first save asks for name and location with a sensible proposal (episode plus language). The source is never modified or overwritten. E2E: open only a source fixture, run the command, every cue and timing is carried over with empty text, translate two lines, save: a new file appears at the chosen path and the source fixture is byte-identical to before.
  - AC (decision 7, subtitles on video): the video shows the **translation**, with a toggle to show the source instead. The preview is fed from a shadow copy in the working folder; the user's file is **never** saved to produce a preview. E2E: with both documents open, pause on a time where a cue is active — the translation text is on the frame; toggle, and the source text is; neither file on disk changes, and no backup is created by toggling or typing.

**Status M2 part A, 2026-08-28:** M2.1-M2.3 on `main`. New crate `sublore-edit` (splice-based mutation planning, verification, undo/redo with explicit run boundaries, edit sessions). 289 Rust tests and 17 E2E checks green; the 2000-cue fixture opens in 68 ms against a 1000 ms budget with 26 rows in the DOM. Review caught and fixed three real defects: ASS cue deletion refused between blank runs, undo coalescing two deliberate edits into one step, and a global Ctrl+Z stealing native undo from text inputs. M2.4-M2.6 not started.

**Owner checklist M2:** open a real subtitle file with its video → edit some lines → adjust a cue against the waveform → undo a few times → save → reopen and confirm your edits are there and nothing else changed. Subtitle a 1-minute clip start to finish without another tool.

## M3 — Local transcription (tasks detailed 2026-08-28)

Goal: whisper.cpp sidecar producing editable, word-timestamped cues.

  - **Closed 2026-09-09, verified on Linux.** Each of the three criteria has a check behind it: `source-column.spec.js` draws the source beside the target row for row and proves the file being read is never written, whatever is done to the one being written; `new-translation.spec.js` carries every cue and timing over with nothing written in them yet and saves to a file of its own; and `preview.spec.js` drives the source-on-video toggle. The shadow copy decision 7 asks for is what the preview feeds mpv from.
- [x] **M3.1 Sidecar process wrapper.** Run whisper.cpp as a child process (never in-process, never on the main thread): spawn, stream progress, cancel, reap. Build/vendor strategy for the binary decided and documented; CUDA never a hard dependency.
  - AC: a behavioral test runs a real transcription of a short audio fixture end to end and gets output; cancelling mid-run kills the child and leaves no orphan process (verified with a process check); the app stays responsive throughout; a missing or unrunnable binary produces a readable error, never a crash.
- [x] **M3.2 Model management.** Explicit, user-initiated model download with resume and integrity check; models live in the app data dir; nothing downloads on its own.
  - AC: no network request happens unless the user asks for a download (CONTRIBUTING.md §1); an interrupted download resumes instead of restarting; a corrupt or truncated file is detected by checksum and refused, never handed to whisper; models are never committed to the repo.
- [x] **M3.3 Word timestamps to cues.** Convert whisper output (word-level timestamps) into subtitle cues in the M1 document model, with a documented, deterministic segmentation rule.
  - AC: transcribing the fixture produces cues whose times are inside the media duration and strictly ordered; the same input always produces the same cues; the result is a valid document that passes the M1 coverage guard and can be saved as SRT and reopened byte-identically.
- [x] **M3.4 Transcription UI.** Choose model, start, see progress, cancel. Runs off the main thread with visible, cancellable progress (CONTRIBUTING.md §7).
  - AC: E2E: start a transcription on the fixture, progress advances, cancel stops it and the UI returns to a usable state with no orphan process; on completion the cues appear in the app; GPU/CPU selection is visible and CPU fallback works with no GPU present.

- [x] **M3.5 A finished transcription becomes the open document (filed 2026-09-01, owner ruling — wave 2).** `TranscribeBar` shows the cues a run produced and the run ends there: the result never becomes the document being edited, so the product's central flow, transcribe and then correct, is cut in half. The owner ruled the behaviour: the transcription becomes the open document. This is the most important of the three reachability holes.
  - AC: a transcription that finishes leaves its cues as the open document, editable in the grid, with the same undo the editor uses, and Save writes them.
  - AC: when a document with unsaved changes is already open, the user is asked the same question the close gate asks — save, discard, cancel — before the transcription replaces it, and cancel leaves both the old document and the transcription result intact (CONTRIBUTING.md §3, decision 9).
  - AC: nothing is written to disk by the transcription itself; the result exists in the session until the user saves it, and the media file is never touched (CONTRIBUTING.md §3.1).
  - AC: E2E drives it end to end: transcribe the fixture, edit a cue of the result, save, reopen, the edit is there.
  - Status 2026-09-01, verified-by-tests **on Linux**: the run keeps the SRT it already rendered, and `subtitle_adopt_transcription` parses it into a session with no path, which `History::unsaved()` makes dirty from birth. Nothing is written by the run; the E2E asserts the save directory is empty and the media untouched. Replacing unsaved work asks the close gate's own three-answer dialog, parameterised rather than copied, with save and replacement under one session lock so a failed save replaces nothing. Four E2E tests added, `EXPECTED_TESTS` 39 to 43; the CPU test gained the dialog answer its new route needs and kept every assertion it had.
  - Owed, and now closed (2026-09-01, decision 24 B2, verified-by-tests **on Linux**): the close gate's Save could not write a document that has no file. It asks for one now, through the save chooser, and writes there; cancelling that chooser still writes nothing and holds the window open with the work in it, which is decision 17's behaviour and the safe direction. The same first save is what the toolbar's Save and Ctrl+S do — the document adopts the path it is given and every save after it writes there, in place and with no chooser. Four E2E tests added, `EXPECTED_TESTS` 43 to 47, including the close gate's chooser driven through the window; the gate's *successful* first save is proved by the command bodies, not by the app, because writing then closing ends the session the suite drives. Two assertions in the M3.5 E2E that pinned the old refusal — Save disabled on a document with no file — now assert it is offered.

**Status 2026-08-28:** M3.1-M3.4 on `main` (developed in a parallel worktree, merged onto the editor and projects). New crate `sublore-asr`: whisper.cpp sidecar built by `scripts/build-whisper.sh` from the commit pinned in `whisper.pin` (never committed), model store with explicit resumable downloads, deterministic word-to-cue segmentation. 489 Rust tests, 27 E2E checks and the real-whisper suite (7 tests against the actual binary and model, transcription, cancellation, progress, determinism) all green. Review caught a model integrity hole: a corrupted model passed the length-only check and made whisper emit silently wrong text with exit 0; `resolve()` now verifies the sha256 and the UI offers a re-download. The pre-commit hook now blocks binaries, models and generated audio from ever entering the repo. Windows and CI unverified.

**Owner checklist M3:** download a model from inside the app → transcribe a short clip → watch progress → cancel one run and confirm nothing is left running → let one finish and see the cues appear, editable.

## M4 — Projects (tasks detailed 2026-08-28)

Goal: SQLite project (series → episodes → files) so memory has somewhere to live.

- [x] **M4.1 Schema and migrations.** One SQLite database file per project; schema for series, episodes, and the files attached to each episode (media path, subtitle paths, role); a versioned migration runner.
  - AC: creating a project produces a database at the chosen path with the current schema version; an automated test takes a database written at version N, migrates it, and verifies both the schema and every row survives (old db → migrate → verify, CONTRIBUTING.md §2); a database from a newer version than the app is refused with a readable error, never silently altered.
- [x] **M4.2 Project lifecycle.** Create, open, close a project; add episodes; attach existing media and subtitle files to an episode by path.
  - AC: create a project, add two episodes with files, close and reopen: everything is still there with the same paths and order; attaching a file records only its path and metadata, never copies or moves the user's file; opening a database that is corrupt or not a Sublore project fails with a readable error and leaves it untouched.
- [x] **M4.3 Deletion safety.** Deleting a project or an episode removes only Sublore's own records and its own project folder contents.
  - AC: a behavioral test with real files on disk deletes a project whose episodes reference media and subtitles outside the project folder, then asserts every one of those user files still exists byte-identical (CONTRIBUTING.md §3); no code path deletes outside the project folder.
- [x] **M4.4 Project UI, minimal.** Create/open a project, see its episodes and their attached files, add an episode, attach a file. No editing beyond that.
  - AC: E2E: create a project in a temp location, add an episode, attach a subtitle fixture, restart the app, reopen the project, the episode and its file are listed; errors surface as readable messages.

- [x] **M4.5 An attached file opens (filed 2026-09-01, owner ruling; closed 2026-09-09).** `ProjectPanel` lists an episode's attached files as text. Nothing happens when one is clicked, so a project is a record of paths rather than a way into the work, and the user still opens every file through the chooser as if the project did not exist.
  - AC: activating an attached file opens it — a subtitle as the document, a video in the player — through the same commands the chooser route uses.
  - AC: a file that has moved or been deleted since it was attached says so in a readable message and changes nothing; the episode keeps its record and the app stays usable.
  - AC: opening a file this way honours the unsaved-changes question exactly as the chooser route does.
  - AC: E2E drives it: attach a subtitle to an episode, activate it, the cues are on screen; delete the file, activate it again, the message appears and the app is still usable.

**Status 2026-08-28:** M4.1-M4.4 on `main` (developed in a parallel worktree, merged onto M2 part A). New crate `sublore-project` (SQLite store, versioned migrations, project lifecycle, deletion safety). 359 Rust tests and 22 E2E checks green after integration. Review caught two critical races in `Database::create`, both reproduced before the fix: a symlink could redirect the project database outside the folder the user chose, and two concurrent creates could delete the winner's finished project; both closed with an atomic claim plus `SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW`. Merge integration moved the project panel into a left sidebar so the cue list keeps its height. Windows and CI unverified.

**Owner checklist M4:** create a project → add an episode → attach a subtitle file and a video → close the app → reopen the project and find everything where you left it → delete the project and confirm your own video and subtitle files are still on disk.

## M5 — Termbase + QA (pro, closed module)

Goal: the product. Per-project glossary with approved renderings; QA pass flags every target line where a source term appears without its approved rendering.
Draft criteria: on the standard fixture episode, QA flags exactly the planted violations and nothing else; term added mid-series retro-flags earlier episodes on demand; module absent → core runs untouched, module present + licensed → features appear.

**Constraint (decision 6, 2026-08-29):** the text matcher and the ASS override-tag scanner live in an **open-core crate**, and M5 consumes it. The closed module keeps only persistence (TM and termbase storage) and QA policy. Search and QA share fixtures. The comparison both need is identical — find a source term in a line while ignoring override tags — and CONTRIBUTING.md §4 requires the open core to be fully useful alone. Two engines would leave the free product unable to search, and the two would disagree about what counts as a match.

**Prerequisite:** N3 must be answered before this milestone starts.

## MW — Windows activation (mandatory before any sale or public release)

Goal: make Windows a verified platform instead of a compiled one. Filed 2026-08-29 with the platform policy change in CONTRIBUTING.md; decision 3 puts the scattered Windows work here rather than inside feature milestones, where half-finished platform work would spread across all of them.

Today the `check` job builds on ubuntu and windows (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:18`), but the behavioural suite runs on ubuntu only (`:125-126`) and drives the app with `xdotool` over XTEST while inspecting windows with `xwininfo` (`e2e/lib/input.js:7-10`) — neither exists on Windows. So every behavioural verdict in this file covers Linux and nothing else.

**What decision 24 G1 means for CI, and it is not free.** There is no separate Windows box and there will not be one: Windows 11 x64 is a dual boot on the owner's own machine, the self-hosted runner is powered up on demand, and MW.4's no-GPU case runs in a KVM virtual machine on Fedora. Hosted `windows-latest` stays a compile-only check on every push. **The Windows behavioural battery therefore runs at gates and tags, not on every push**, so a Windows regression can be up to a milestone old by the time anything catches it. Two consequences to plan around rather than discover: the commit that broke Windows has to be found by bisecting a milestone's worth of history, and Linux stays the only platform where a red build points at the change that made it red.

  - **Closed 2026-09-09, verified on Linux: built and never ticked, the third such entry found in one triage.** `ProjectRail.activate` opens the file and `App.openAttachedFile` routes it, a media through `open` and a subtitle through `subtitle.open`, which are the very functions the chooser route calls, so the third criterion holds by construction rather than by a second implementation: neither route flushes an open editor first, so both ask the unsaved question the same way. A record whose file has gone opens Locate instead of failing, which is the second criterion and better than a message. `project.spec.js` carries both: "opens an attached subtitle from the rail" and "marks an attachment whose file has gone and points it at the file again".
- [x] **MW.1a Say which part of the harness is Linux, and give the rest a seam (done 2026-09-02, verified on Linux).** Every file under `e2e/lib` and `e2e/tools` read and sorted into portable, Linux by nature, or Linux by accident. The Linux-by-nature ones now refuse by name on any other platform through `e2e/lib/platform.js`'s `requireLinuxBackend(seam, owes)`, each naming what a Windows counterpart has to do, so MW.1b fills a seam in rather than restructuring around one. No Windows code was written: none of it could have been run, and an unrun implementation reported as done is what the platform policy forbids.
  - **Portable already:** `e2e/lib/applog.js` (the data home arrives as an argument), `e2e/lib/tally.js`, `e2e/lib/env.js` (portable mechanism, and all three of its values are inert on Windows: nothing there reads `GDK_BACKEND` or `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, and `main.rs` gates the webkit hatch behind `cfg(target_os = "linux")`), `e2e/tools/whisper-stub.mjs` (plain Node; only the shebang that runs it is Linux), and the new `e2e/lib/platform.js`. `waitFor` in `e2e/lib/proc.js` belongs here too.
  - **Linux by nature, seamed for MW.1b:** `e2e/lib/x11.js` (all of it, at the `xwininfo` chokepoint), `e2e/lib/input.js` (all of it, at the `xdotool` chokepoint), `e2e/lib/proc.js` `processGroupMembers` and `killGroup` (POSIX process groups), `e2e/lib/driver.js` `startDriver` (WebKitWebDriver, plus the process group `stopDriver` kills through), `e2e/lib/pixels.js` `screenGrabArgs` (only the `x11grab` input; the saturation measure above it is the same anywhere), `e2e/lib/asr.js` `appDataDir`, `installStubSidecar` and `processLine`, and `e2e/tools/close-window.py` (an ICCCM `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` ClientMessage).
  - **Linux by nature, no seam of their own on purpose:** `e2e/lib/gtk-dialog.js` and `e2e/lib/chooser.js`. Their window-finding half is generic and their keystrokes are GTK's (Alt mnemonics, Alt+Home, Ctrl+L, Alt+O); every path through them reaches `x11.js` or `input.js` first, so a second guard would be noise rather than safety. Both headers now say what MW.1b owes them.
  - **Linux by accident, fixed here:** one, in `e2e/lib/asr.js`. The model lookup falls back to `$HOME/.cache` and its failure message named only `XDG_CACHE_HOME` and `HOME`, neither of which Windows sets; it now names `SUBLORE_TEST_MODEL_DIR`, which is the platform-neutral way in and already existed.
  - AC, met: `pnpm lint` and `pnpm format:check` exit 0; the full wdio suite (9 spec files, 53 tests) and every script check that imports a touched library are green on Linux; `pixels.js`'s `saturation()` returns the same number before and after the extraction against the same painted display (118 on red, 0 on black); and all nine seams were made to fire, each with its own message. Verified on Linux, on Fedora with Xvfb and on the owner's Wayland session. No Windows result is claimed.
- [ ] **MW.1b The Windows implementations behind those seams.** Needs the dual boot of decision 24 G1 to exist, because every item is written by running it. Window inspection to replace `xwininfo` (toplevels by name and geometry, map state, children, and the app's own window picked out of them); synthetic input to replace `xdotool` over XTEST (real key, button and pointer-motion events, and moving the input focus); a process-group equivalent for `processGroupMembers` and `killGroup` (a job object is the unit); `startDriver` against the WebView2 driver rather than WebKitWebDriver, stopping the whole tree; a Windows screen grab for `webview-paint-check.js`, which reads the app's own window rather than the root, so `import` and the X11 grab both need a replacement; a shim the app can spawn as `SUBLORE_WHISPER_BIN`, since a `.mjs` has no shebang there; `processLine` without `ps`, still able to tell a reaped child from an unreaped one; a `WM_CLOSE` sibling for `close-window.py`; and Windows key tables for `gtk-dialog.js` and `chooser.js`, which face `dialog.rs`'s non-Linux arm and the common item dialog rather than GTK.
  - **2026-09-09: one seam on the closed list above is gone with its file.** `e2e/lib/pixels.js` was deleted: `saturation()` had no caller and `requireFfmpeg()` duplicated `requireTool`. The list in the closed entry is the record of what was seamed then, not a list of files to open now.
  - Also owed, in files MW.1a did not own: `e2e/lib/paths.js` needs `appBinary` to carry `.exe`, `requireTool` to stop shelling out to `sh -c command -v`, `requireDisplay` to mean something other than `DISPLAY`, and `closeWindowTool` to become the place that picks between the two close tools. `firstCueText` is a coordinate measured on one Linux window and will need its own Windows measurement.
  - AC: the full behavioural suite runs on Windows and is green, with the same spec files and the same assertions as on Linux. A failure on either platform turns the gate red.
- [ ] **MW.1c Redirect the app's data home on Windows.** The harness points a run at a throwaway data home by setting `XDG_DATA_HOME`, which is how `wdio.conf.js`, the close-gate checks, `applog.js` and `asr.js` all find what the app wrote. Windows resolves `app_data_dir()` from a known folder id and no environment variable moves it, so on Windows those checks would read the real profile or nothing at all. Whatever replaces it touches `src-tauri` and `e2e/wdio.conf.js`, so it is its own change and not a harness edit.
  - AC: a Windows run writes its logs, backups and scratch under a directory the run created and deletes, and a check can read them from there. Running the suite twice leaves nothing behind in the user's profile.
- [ ] **MW.2 Platform hardening.** Work through what only Windows can show: the native video surface z-order (`video/surface/windows.rs` reasserts `HWND_TOP`), path and encoding handling, the crash dialog, the installer.
  - AC: the M0.2, M0.4, N1, N2 and M2.0 criteria are re-run on Windows and pass there, including the occlusion behaviour of decision 1.
- [ ] **MW.4 The dependency graph on a machine without a GPU driver.** `libmpv-2.dll` imports `vulkan-1.dll` statically, not through `GetProcAddress`, so the loader has to resolve it before `main` runs even though Sublore never asks mpv for Vulkan. A Windows machine whose only display adapter is the Microsoft Basic one may have no Vulkan loader at all, and the app would die at startup with no message. Filed 2026-08-31 while diagnosing the `STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND` exits of the three Windows CI test targets that link mpv; the runner turned out to have a complete loader, so this is untested rather than known-broken.
  - AC: on a Windows install with no GPU vendor driver, Sublore starts, opens a subtitle and plays a video, or says what is missing in a sentence the user can act on. A silent death at load is a failure.

- [ ] **MW.3 Owner checklist on Windows.** Every owner checklist in this file, run by the owner on a Windows machine from an installed build.
  - AC: the owner signs off. Until then no build ships to anyone.

**This milestone gated release until 2026-09-04, when the owner ruled that v1.0 ships on Linux alone.** It still gates a Windows build: nothing is sold or handed to anyone as a Windows product while it is open. The Linux tag no longer waits for it. See the platform policy in CONTRIBUTING.md.

## M6 — Translation memory + licensing + release

Goal: exact and fuzzy TM across the whole project; offline license check; pay-once purchase flow via merchant of record; v1.0 tag.
Draft criteria: repeated lines in later episodes surface their earlier translation; fuzzy matches ranked and insertable; license file validates offline, invalid license degrades gently to free core; both installers pass the full owner checklist end to end.

**Prerequisite:** none on MW since the 2026-09-04 ruling. The v1.0 Linux tag no longer waits for Windows activation; a Windows build still does.

---

## Parking lot (explicitly not v1 — do not pull forward)

- **Should the CI runner have a minimal window manager? (parked by owner ruling 2026-08-31.)** The first real CI run failed `close-gate-check.js` on `XSetInputFocus` answering `BadMatch`, which is what a bare X server with nothing to hold focus looks like. **For:** users have a window manager, so a runner without one tests an environment nobody has, and focus, stacking and close buttons all behave differently there. **Against:** several checks were built against the WM-less runner and read its behaviour as the baseline — the close gate drives `WM_DELETE_WINDOW` directly, the surface checks read raw stacking — so adding one reopens every one of them at once. Not decided inside gate 2 for that reason. Whoever picks it up starts by listing which checks change meaning under a WM, because that list is the cost.

- **"Still saving" indicator after a threshold (decision 17, 2026-08-31).** The close gate waits on the session lock without a timeout, by ruling, because every automatic release costs the user's work. What is owed is not a bound but a voice: past some threshold the window says it is still saving, so an unbounded wait stops being a silent one.

- **External VAD with timestamp remapping (decision 16, 2026-08-30).** Cue-boundary tuning, done outside whisper.cpp and mapped back onto the transcript's timings. The measured lever is segmentation, not the model and not source separation (`asr-anime.md`), but the built-in Silero is ruled out for this domain, so the instrument is external. Post-v1 pipeline task. **No proof of concept before the parking lot promotes it.**
- **Chinese punctuation as a post-ASR pass (decision 15, 2026-08-30).** Beside re-punctuation. The one shippable in-architecture win the sweep found was a punctuating Mandarin fine-tune, and decision 15 declines it: a per-language model is a UI, a download, an in-app licence and a verification matrix.

Karaoke/typesetting · diarization · built-in LLM translation beyond BYOK hook · macOS activation · auto-updater · batch CLI · Rust-side localized strings for the native crash dialog (v1 ships it English-only, owner decision 2026-08-23). Anything anyone wants to add lands here first and waits for the owner.
